(\Theta_{n,n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})}(\pi_{\rho,k,h}))^{c}=\Theta_{n,n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c})}(\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c})
;

•

If
n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})=\lfloor n+|k|\rfloor
, then
\Theta_{n,n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})=\pi_{\rho,k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
with
k^{\prime}=\lceil|k|\rceil
and
|h^{\prime}|=|h|
;

•

If
n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})=\lfloor n-|k|\rfloor
, then
\Theta_{n,n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})=\pi_{\rho,k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
with
k^{\prime}=\lceil|k|-1\rceil
and
|h^{\prime}|=|h|
.

(ii) Let
\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}(\mathrm{O}_{2n+1})
. Consider the Witt tower
{\bf O}^{\epsilon}_{\mathrm{odd}}
. Then the following hold:

•

(Conservation relation)
\{n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}),n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,-\iota})\}=\{n+k+1,n-k\}
.

•

If
n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=n+k+1
, then
\Theta_{n,n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})}^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=\pi_{\rho,k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
with
k^{\prime}=h
and
|h^{\prime}|=|k+1|
;

•

If
n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=n-k
, then
\Theta_{n,n^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})}^{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=\pi_{\rho,k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
with
k^{\prime}=h
and
|h^{\prime}|=|k|
.

Let
\pi
be an irreducible basic representation of
\mathrm{O}_{2n+1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. We will demonstrate that the first occurrence index is determined solely by
\pi
, regardless of the Witt tower it is located in.

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
\pi\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}_{2n+1})
. Then
n^{+}(\pi)=n^{-}(\pi)
.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
\pi\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
. Then
\pi(-I)=\pi^{c}(-I)
, where
I
is the identity of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.

Proof.

We prove Theorem
4.12
and Lemma
4.13
simultaneously by induction on
n
.
First, we prove Lemma
4.13
for
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}
. Assume that theta correspondence of
\pi=\pi_{\rho,k,h}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
goes down with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{\epsilon}
, the first occurrence index
n^{\epsilon}(\pi)=n^{\prime}

and
\Theta^{\epsilon}_{n,n^{\prime}}(\pi)=\pi^{\prime}
. By Proposition
4.10
(ii), we know that
\pi^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}_{2n^{\prime}+1})
and
n^{\epsilon}(\pi^{\prime})=n
.

If
n=n^{\prime}
, according to Proposition
4.10
, we have
h=0
. Therefore, according to Corollary
3.5
,
\pi=\pi^{c}
. Now, let us assume that
n^{\prime}<n
.
Utilizing the induction assumption on
\pi^{\prime}
, we get
n^{-\epsilon}(\pi^{\prime})=n^{\epsilon}(\pi^{\prime})=n
. However, for the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{-\epsilon}
, the theta lifting of
\pi^{\prime}
to
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is no longer
\pi
, because by Proposition
4.10
(ii),
\pi
goes up with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{-\epsilon}
. Thus, from Proposition
4.11
(ii), it follows that the above mentioned theta lifting of
\pi^{\prime}
has to be
\pi^{c}=\pi_{\rho,k,-h}
. Hence, by Proposition
4.5
,

\pi^{c}(-I)\cdot\pi^{\prime}(-I_{\mathrm{O}_{2n^{\prime}+1}})=\pi(-I)\cdot\pi^{\prime}(-I_{\mathrm{O}_{2n^{\prime}+1}}),

where
I_{\mathrm{O}_{2n^{\prime}+1}}
is the identity of
\mathrm{O}_{2n^{\prime}+1}
. This completes the proof of Lemma
4.13
.

We turn to prove Theorem
4.12
for
\mathrm{O}_{2n+1}
. Let
\pi^{\star}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}_{2n+1})
. Our objective is to check whether
\pi^{\star}
goes up or down simultaneously with respect to two Witt towers
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{\pm}
.
Assume that
\pi^{\star}
goes down with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{\epsilon}

and
\Theta(\pi^{\star})=\pi^{\star\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n^{\star\prime}})
be the first theta lifting. For the Witten tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{-\epsilon}
, the representation
\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi^{\star}
can not go down, because if
\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi^{\star}
goes down, then by Proposition
4.11
(ii), its first theta lifting is either
\pi^{\star\prime}
or
(\pi^{\star\prime})^{c}
. However, by Lemma
4.13
, we have

(\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi^{\star})(-I_{\mathrm{O}_{2n+1}})\cdot\pi^{\star\prime}(-I)=(\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi^{\star})(-I_{\mathrm{O}_{2n+1}})\cdot(\pi^{\star\prime})^{c}(-I)\neq\pi^{\star}(-I_{\mathrm{O}_{2n+1}})\cdot\pi^{\star\prime}(-I)=\varepsilon(-1)^{n^{\star\prime}},

which contradicts Proposition
4.5
.
So
\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi^{\star}
goes up and
\pi^{\star}
goes down with respect to the Witt towers
{\bf{O}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{-\epsilon}
.

If
\pi^{\star}
goes up with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{\epsilon}
, then by Proposition
4.11
(ii),
\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi^{\star}
goes down. Applying the same argument to
\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi^{\star}
, we can conclude that
\pi^{\star}
goes up with respect to the Witt towers
{\bf{O}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{-\epsilon}
.
∎

Corollary 4.14
.

(1)

Let
\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}_{2n+1})
. Then

n^{\pm}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}n+(k+1)&\textrm{ if }\iota={\widetilde{\iota}}_{\rho}\cdot(-1)^{k+1}\\
n-k&\textrm{ if }\iota={\widetilde{\iota}}_{\rho}\cdot(-1)^{k}.\end{array}\right.

(2)

Let
\pi_{\rho,k,h}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
. Then

\pi_{\rho,k,h}(-I)=\pi_{\rho,k,-h}(-I)=\iota_{\rho}\cdot(-1)^{\lfloor|h|\rfloor}\varepsilon(-1)^{\lceil|h|\rceil}.

Proof.

We will use induction on
n
to prove both (1) and (2) at the same time. First, we will prove (i). By Proposition
4.11
(ii), we know that
\{n^{\pm}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}),n^{\pm}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,-\iota})=\{n+(k+1),n-k\}
. WOLG, assume that
n^{\pm}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=n-k
. Then
\Theta^{\pm}_{n,n-k}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=\pi_{\rho,k^{\prime},h^{\pm\prime}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
with
k^{\prime}=h
and
h^{\pm\prime}\in\{\pm k\}\in\mathbb{Z}
. By Proposition
4.5
, we get

\iota=\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}(-I)=\varepsilon(-1)^{n-k}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k^{\prime},h^{\pm\prime}}(-I)=\varepsilon(-1)^{|\rho|+k^{\prime}(k^{\prime}+1)+(h^{\pm\prime})^{2}}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k^{\prime},h^{\pm\prime}}(-I).

Note that we have the following equations:
{\widetilde{\iota}}_{\rho}=\iota_{\rho}\cdot\varepsilon(-1)^{|\rho|}
, and
\varepsilon(-1)^{k^{\prime}(k^{\prime}+1)}=1
and
\varepsilon(-1)^{(h^{\pm\prime})^{2}}=\varepsilon(-1)^{h^{\pm\prime}}
. Hence, (1) follows from our induction assumption on
\mathrm{Sp}_{2(n-k)}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.

The proof of (2) is similar. We pick a Witt Tower
{\bf{O}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{\epsilon}
such that
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
goes down. Then (2) follows from the same see-saw argument and our induction assumption on
\mathrm{O}_{2(n-k)+1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.

∎

5.
Gan-Gross-Prasad problem

From now until the end of subsection
5.2
, we assume that
\mathbb{F}_{q}
is a finite field of odd characteristic.

Let
V_{\mathfrak{n}}
be an
{\mathfrak{n}}
-dimensional
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-vector space endowed with a
\epsilon
-symmetric form
\langle,\rangle_{V_{{\mathfrak{n}}}}
. Suppose that
W\subset V_{{\mathfrak{n}}}
is a non-degenerate subspace with dimensional
{\mathfrak{m}}
satisfying:

•

\epsilon\cdot(-1)^{\mathrm{dim}W^{\bot}}=-1
,

•

W^{\bot}
is a split space.

According to whether
{\mathfrak{n}}-{\mathfrak{m}}
is odd or even, Gan-Gross-Prasad problem is called the Bessel case or Fourier-Jacobi case.

5.1.
Bessel case

Let
V_{\mathfrak{n}}
be a symmetric space with
{\mathrm{disc}}(V_{\mathfrak{n}})=\epsilon
.
Consider a decomposition of
V_{\mathfrak{n}}
:

V_{\mathfrak{n}}=X+V_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell}+X^{\vee}

where
X+X^{\vee}=V_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell}^{\perp}
is a polarization. Pick a basis
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{\ell}\}
of
X
. Let
P
be the parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{\mathfrak{n}}
stabilizing the flag

\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\{e_{1}\}\subset\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\{e_{1},e_{2}\}\subset\cdots\subset\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\{e_{1},\cdots,e_{\ell}\}.

The Levi component of
P
is
M\cong\mathrm{GL}_{1}^{\ell}\times\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell}
. Its unipotent radical can be written in the form

N_{\ell}=\left\{n=\begin{pmatrix}z&y&x\\
0&I_{n-2\ell}&y^{\prime}\\
0&0&z^{*}\end{pmatrix}:z\in U_{\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}}\right\},

where the superscript
∗
denotes the transpose inverse, and
U_{\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}}
is the subgroup of unipotent upper triangular matrices of
\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}
.
Define a generic character
\psi_{v_{0}}
of
N_{\ell}
by

\psi_{v_{0}}(n)=\psi\left(\sum^{\ell-1}_{i=1}z_{i,i+1}+\langle y_{\ell},v_{0}\rangle_{V_{{\mathfrak{n}}}}\right),\quad n\in N_{\ell}(\mathbb{F}_{q}),

where
y_{\ell}
is the last row of
y
, and
v_{0}\in V_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell}
is an anisotropic vector. Let
W
be the orthogonal complement of
v_{0}
in
V_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell}
. Assume that
{\mathrm{disc}}(W)=\epsilon^{\prime}
.
The stabilizer of
\psi_{v_{0}}
in
M(\mathbb{F}_{q})
is the orthogonal group
\mathrm{O}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
.
Put

(5.1)

H=\mathrm{O}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}\ltimes N_{\ell}.

Let
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
be two irreducible cuspidal representations of
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{{\mathfrak{n}}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
and
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
respectively. The Gan-Gross-Prasad problem is concerned with the multiplicity:

(5.2)

m_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{v_{0}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime}):=\dim\mathrm{Hom}_{H(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi,\pi^{\prime}\otimes\psi_{v_{0}})=\dim\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{O}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi^{\prime},\mathrm{Hom}_{N_{\ell}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi,\psi_{v_{0}})),

where
\epsilon_{v_{0}}={\mathrm{disc}}(\langle v_{0}\rangle)
.
If
\ell=0
, then

m_{\epsilon^{\prime}_{v_{0}}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime}):=\langle\pi,\pi^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathrm{O}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-1}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})},

and we call the above multiplicity the basic case for Bessel case.
Let
v_{0}
,
v_{0}^{*}\in V_{n-2\ell}
be two anisotropic vectors such that
\langle v_{0},v_{0}\rangle_{V_{\mathfrak{n}}}/\langle v_{0}^{*},v_{0}^{*}\rangle_{V_{\mathfrak{n}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}/(\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times})^{2}
. Let
W^{*}
be the orthogonal complement of
v_{0}^{*}
in
V_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell}
. Assume that
{\mathrm{disc}}(W^{*})=\epsilon^{*}
. Then the stabilizer of
\psi_{v_{0}^{*}}
is
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{*}}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}
. If
{\mathfrak{n}}
is even, then
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}
and
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{*}}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}
are the same as abstract groups; however they act on two symmetric spaces with different discriminants, and
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}
,
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{*}}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}
are not conjugate in
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell}
. If
{\mathfrak{n}}
is odd, then one of
\{W,W^{*}\}
is split and the other is not. Thus we get
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}\ncong\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{*}}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell-1}
in this case.

Define the
first descent index

{\ell}_{0}:={\ell}_{0}^{\mathrm{B}}(\pi)
of
\pi
in the Bessel case to be the largest nonnegative integer
{\ell}_{0}<{\mathfrak{n}}/2
such that
m_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\neq 0
for some irreducible representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell_{0}-1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
and some
{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}\in\{\pm\}
. Define the
first descent
of
\pi
with respect to
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
to be

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell_{0},\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\pi):=\mathrm{Hom}_{N_{\ell_{0}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi,\psi_{v_{0}})

where
v_{0}\in V_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell_{0}}
is an anisotropic vector such that
{\mathrm{disc}}(\langle v_{0}\rangle)=\epsilon^{\prime\prime}
.

5.2.
Fourier-Jacobi case

We next turn to the Fourier-Jacobi case. Let
{\mathfrak{n}}=2n
, and
V_{2n}
be a symplectic space.
Consider pairs of symplectic spaces
V_{2(n-\ell)}\subset V_{2n}
, and the decomposition

V_{n}=X+V_{2(n-\ell)}+X^{\vee}

We use similar notations for various subspaces and subgroups as in the Bessel case. Note that if we let
P
be the parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}
stabilizing
X
and let
N
be its unipotent radical, then
N_{\ell}=U_{\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}}\ltimes N
. Let
\omega_{n-\ell}^{\epsilon}
be the Weil representation of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2(n-\ell)}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\ltimes\mathcal{H}_{2(n-\ell)}
, where
\mathcal{H}_{2(n-\ell)}
is the Heisenberg group of
V_{2(n-\ell)}
. Roughly speaking, there is a natural homomorphism
N(\mathbb{F}_{q})\to\mathcal{H}_{2(n-\ell)}
invariant under the conjugation action of
U_{\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
on
N(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, which enables us to view
\omega_{n-\ell}^{\epsilon}
as a representation of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2(n-\ell)}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\ltimes N_{\ell}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Let
\psi^{\epsilon}_{\ell}
be the character of
U_{\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
given by

\psi^{\epsilon}_{\ell}(z)=\psi\left(a\sum^{\ell-1}_{i=1}z_{i,i+1}\right),\quad z\in U_{\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}}(\mathbb{F}_{q}),\textrm{ and }\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}a=1&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=+;\\
a\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}/\penalty(\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times})^{2}&\textrm{ if }\epsilon=-.\\
\end{array}\right.

For the Fourier-Jacobi case, put

(5.3)

H=\mathrm{Sp}_{2(n-\ell)}\ltimes N_{\ell}.

Similar to the Bessel case, the Gan-Gross-Prasad problem is concerned with the multiplicity:

(5.4)

m_{\epsilon}(\pi,\pi^{\prime}):=\mathrm{dim}\mathrm{Hom}_{H(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi,\pi^{\prime}\otimes(\omega_{n-\ell}^{\epsilon}\otimes\psi^{\epsilon}_{\ell}))=\dim\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2(n-\ell)(\mathbb{F}_{q})}}(\pi^{\prime},\mathrm{Hom}_{N_{\ell}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi,\omega_{n-\ell}^{\epsilon}\otimes\psi^{\epsilon}_{\ell})).

If
\ell=0
, then

m_{\epsilon}(\pi,\pi^{\prime}):=\langle\pi,\pi^{\prime}\otimes\omega^{\epsilon}_{n}\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})},

and we call the above multiplicity the basic case for Bessel case.

Define the
first descent index

{\ell}_{0}:={\ell}_{0}^{\mathrm{FJ}}(\pi)
of
\pi
in the Fourier-Jacobi case to be the largest nonnegative integer
{\ell}_{0}<n
such that
m_{\epsilon}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\neq 0
for some irreducible representation
\pi^{\prime}
of
\mathrm{Sp}_{{\mathfrak{n}}-2\ell_{0}}
and some
\epsilon\in\{\pm\}
. Define the
first descent
of
\pi
with respect to
\epsilon
to be

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell_{0},\epsilon}(\pi):=\mathrm{Hom}_{N_{\ell_{0}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\pi,\omega_{n-\ell_{0}}^{\epsilon}\otimes\psi^{\epsilon}_{\ell_{0}}).

5.3.
Reduction to the basic case

From now until the end of this section, we assume that the cardinality of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
is large
enough so that the main result in
[
Sr1
]
holds.
We first show that parabolic induction preserves multiplicities, and thereby make a reduction to the basic case.

Proposition 5.1
.

[
Wang1
, Proposition 7.3]

Let be a semisimple element of
(G^{*})^{F}=\mathrm{SO}_{2n+1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, and
s^{\prime}
be a semisimple element of
(\mathrm{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{F}_{q})^{*})^{F}=\mathrm{SO}_{2m+1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Let
\pi\in\cal{E}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n},s)
be an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, and let
\pi^{\prime}\in\cal{E}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2m},s^{\prime})
be an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2m}
with
n\geq m
. Let
P
be an
F
-stable maximal parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}
with Levi factor
\mathrm{GL}_{n-m}\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2m}
. Let
s_{0}
be a semisimple element of
\mathrm{GL}_{n-m}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
and let
\tau\in\cal{E}(\mathrm{GL}_{n-m},s_{0})
be an irreducible cuspidal representation of
\mathrm{GL}_{n-m}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
which is nontrivial if
n-m=1
. Assume that
s_{0}
has no common eigenvalues with
s
and
s^{\prime}
. Then we have

(5.5)

m_{\epsilon}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=\langle\pi\otimes\omega_{n}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon},\mathrm{Ind}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}.

We also have similar result for Bessel case.

Proposition 5.2
.

[
Wang1
, Proposition 7.4, Corollary 7.5]

Let
s
be a semisimple element of
\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon}_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, and
s^{\prime}
be a semisimple element of
\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Let
\pi\in\cal{E}(\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon}_{n},s)
be an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon}_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, and let
\pi^{\prime}\in\cal{E}(\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{m},s^{\prime})
be an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
with
n>m
,
n\equiv m+1
mod
2
. Let
P
be an
F
-stable maximal parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{n+1}
with Levi factor
\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}\times\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{m}
,
\ell=(n+1-m)/2
. Let
s_{0}
be a semisimple element of
\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Let
\tau_{1}
(resp.
\tau_{2}
) be an irreducible cuspidal representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
(resp.
\mathrm{GL}_{\ell-\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q}))
,
\ell^{\prime}\leq\ell
, which is nontrivial if
\ell^{\prime}=1
(resp.
\ell-\ell^{\prime}=1
), and

\tau=\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathrm{GL}_{\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\times\mathrm{GL}_{\ell-\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}^{\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2}).

Assume that
\tau\in\cal{E}(\mathrm{GL}_{\ell},s_{0})
, and
s_{0}
has no common eigenvalues with
s
and
s^{\prime}
. Then we have

(5.6)

m_{\varepsilon(-1)^{\prime}{\epsilon^{\prime}}\epsilon}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})=\langle\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{n+1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi^{\prime}),\pi\rangle_{\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon}_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}=m_{\varepsilon(-1)^{\prime}\varepsilon(-1){\epsilon^{\prime}}\epsilon}\left(\pi,\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{n+1-2\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{\mathrm{GL}_{\ell-\ell^{\prime}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})\times\mathrm{SO}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{m}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau_{2}\otimes\pi^{\prime})\right),

where

\varepsilon(-1)^{\prime}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}+&\textrm{ if $n$ is odd};\\
\varepsilon(-1)&\textrm{ if $n$ is even}.\\
\end{array}\right.

Remark 5.3
.

Recall that we assume that the order
q
of finite filed
\mathbb{F}_{q}
is large enough such that the main theorem in
[
Sr1
]
holds. This assumption guarantees the existence of
\tau
, which meets the conditions outlined in Proposition
5.2
. For a quadratic unipotent representation
\pi
, we only need to assume that
q
is odd for
G
to ensure the existence of
\tau
. In other words, Proposition
5.1
and Proposition
5.2
apply to quadratic unipotent representation when
q
is odd. Moreover, in
[
Wang1
]
, the assumption of
q
is only for obtaining Proposition
5.1
and Proposition
5.2
. Therefore, all calculations of Gan-Gross-Prasad in
[
Wang1
]
apply to quadratic unipotent representations when
q
is odd.

In order to apply the theta correspondence we will work with orthogonal groups instead of special orthogonal groups. In Proposition
5.2
, for
m=0
, assume that
\tau\in\cal{E}(\mathrm{GL}_{\ell},s_{0})
such that
\pm 1
are not eigenvalues of
s_{0}
.
Let
\bf{1}
denotes the trivial representation of trivial group. By
[
Wang1
, Proposition 3.2]
, we set

m_{\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}\varepsilon(-1)^{\prime}}(\pi,{\bf{1}}):=\langle\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{n+1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau),\pi\rangle_{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\textrm{ if }\pi\textrm{ is regular };\\
0,&\textrm{ otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
\varepsilon(-1)^{\prime}
is defined in Proposition
5.2
.
By the standard arguments of theta correspondence and see-saw dual pairs, we set

m_{\epsilon}(\pi,{\bf{1}}):=\langle\pi\otimes\omega_{n}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon},\mathrm{Ind}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau)\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\textrm{ if }\pi\textrm{ is regular };\\
0,&\textrm{ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

5.4.
Strongly relevant pair of representations

For a pair of irreducible representations
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
, whether the multiplicity (
5.2
) and (
5.4
) vanishes depends on the behavior of the pair in see-saw.

Example 5.4
.

Let
\pi
be a unipotent cuspidal representations of
\mathrm{O}^{+}_{5}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. By
[
LW1
, Theorem 3.12]
, there exists a representation
\pi_{0}\in[\pi]
such that
\Theta^{+}_{2,1}(\pi_{0})=\pi_{1}
where
\pi_{1}
is a cuspidal representation of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Let
\pi^{\prime}\in\cal{E}(\mathrm{O}^{+}_{4},s)
where
s
has no eigenvalues
\pm 1
. Then by Theorem
4.3
, the first occurrence index of
\pi^{\prime}
is
2
, and
\Theta^{+}_{2,2}(\pi^{\prime})=\pi^{\prime}_{1}
where
\pi^{\prime}_{1}
is an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{Sp}_{4}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Consider the see-saw diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 22.48618pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.48618pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2}\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 57.58344pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{+}_{5}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-10.79863pt\raise-32.40671pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 46.48618pt\raise-32.40671pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{+}_{4}\times\mathrm{O}^{+}_{1}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We have

m_{+}(\pi_{0},\pi^{\prime})=\langle\pi_{0},\pi^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathrm{O}^{+}_{4}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}\leq\langle\Theta^{+}_{1,2}(\pi_{1}),\pi^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathrm{O}^{+}_{4}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}=\langle\pi_{1},\Theta^{+}_{2,1}(\pi^{\prime})\otimes\omega_{1}^{+}\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}=0.

We need to choose appropriate pairs of representations to avoid the above situation. These pairs of irreducible representations are called strongly relevant or
(\psi,\varepsilon(-1))
-strongly relevant, as defined in
[
Wang1
, subsection 6.3]
. Although the definitions might appear complex, we will only use them for basic representations. In such cases, the definition is quite straightforward. In this paper, we always fix a character
\psi
and write weil representations of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
by
\omega_{n}^{\pm}
instead of
\omega_{n,\psi}
and
\omega_{n,\psi^{\prime}}
, where
\psi^{\prime}(x)=\psi(ax)
with
x\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}
and
a\notin(\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times})^{2}
. So we use
\{\pm\}
-strongly relevant instead of
(\psi,\varepsilon(-1))
and
(\psi^{\prime},\varepsilon(-1))
-strongly relevant.

Definition 5.5
.

(1)

Let
\pi\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
, and
\pi^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2m})
. Pick
\epsilon^{\prime}\in\{\pm\}
such that
\pi
goes down with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{even}}
. We say the pair of representations
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
is
\epsilon
-relevant if
{\bf n}_{\mathrm{even}}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}(\pi)={\bf n}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}}(\pi^{\prime})-1
or
{\bf n}_{\mathrm{even}}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}(\pi)={\bf n}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon\epsilon^{\prime}}(\pi^{\prime})
. We say the pair of representations
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
is
\epsilon
-strongly relevant if
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
is
\epsilon
-relevant and
(\pi^{\prime},\pi)
is
\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon
-relevant.

(2)

Let
\pi\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n+1})
, and let
\pi^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2m})
.
Pick
\chi\in\{1,\mathrm{sgn}\}
such that
\chi\cdot\pi
goes down with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf Sp}
. Let
\chi^{\prime}
be a character of
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2m}
such that

\chi^{\prime}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\textrm{ if }\chi=1;\\
\mathrm{sgn}&\textrm{ if }\chi=\mathrm{sgn}.\end{array}\right.

We say the pair of representations
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
is relevant if

{\bf{n}}^{\epsilon}(\chi\cdot\pi)={\bf{n}}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}(\chi^{\prime}\cdot\pi^{\prime})-1\textrm{ or }{\bf{n}}^{\epsilon}(\chi\cdot\pi)={\bf{n}}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}(\chi^{\prime}\cdot\pi^{\prime})
.
We say the pair of representations
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
is strongly relevant if both
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
and
(\chi_{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n+1}}\otimes\pi,\chi_{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2n^{\prime}}}\otimes\pi^{\prime})
are relevant where
\chi_{G}
is the spinor norm of
G^{F}
.

Example 5.6
.

Consider the following two see-saw diagrams

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 27.4295pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.4295pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.20288pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 144.8884pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\quad\quad}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 194.88843pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 281.17107pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n^{\prime}+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.2703pt\raise-32.30057pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 51.4295pt\raise-32.30057pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\varepsilon(-1)\cdot\epsilon}_{2n^{\prime}+1}\times\mathrm{O}^{\varepsilon(-1)}_{1}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 144.8884pt\raise-32.30057pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\quad\quad}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 209.04764pt\raise-32.30057pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 273.74744pt\raise-32.30057pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n^{\prime}}\times\mathrm{O}^{+}_{1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

As in Example
5.4
, we can conclude that if
(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
is not a
\varepsilon(-1)
-strongly relevant pair, then the multiplicity
m_{+}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
vanishes. In particular, if the multiplicity
m_{+}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})
does not vanish, then
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
have to go up or down simultaneously in the same see-saw diagram.

The
\epsilon
-relevant pairs for cuspidal representations have been described in
[
Wang1
, Section 6]
. It is not difficult to extend these results to basic representations.

Corollary 5.7
.

[
Wang1
, Corollary 6.6]

Let
\pi_{\rho,k,h}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
, and
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2m})
.

(i) If
(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})
is
\epsilon
-relevant for some
\epsilon\in\{\pm\}
, then
k=\lfloor|h^{\prime}|\rfloor
or
k=\lceil|h^{\prime}|-1\rceil
.

(ii) If
(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})
is
\epsilon
-relevant for some
\epsilon\in\{\pm\}
, then
(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho,k^{\prime},-h^{\prime}})
is not
\epsilon
-relevant.

(iii) If
(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})
is not
\epsilon
-relevant for some
\epsilon\in\{\pm\}
and
k=\lfloor|h^{\prime}|\rfloor
or
k=\lceil|h^{\prime}|-1\rceil
, then
(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho,k^{\prime},-h^{\prime}})
is
\epsilon
-relevant.

Corollary 5.8
.

[
Wang1
, Corollary 6.7]

(i) Let
\pi_{\rho,k,h}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n})
, and
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2m+1})
. If
(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}})
is relevant, then
\lfloor|k|\rfloor=k^{\prime}
or
\lceil|k|-1\rceil=k^{\prime}
.

(ii) Let
\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n+1})
, and
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2m})
. If
(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})
is relevant, then
\lfloor|k^{\prime}|\rfloor=k
or
\lceil|k^{\prime}|\rceil=k+1
.

5.5.
First descent of basic representation

Consider an irreducible cuspidal representation
\pi
. We now turn to calculate the first descent of representations in
[\pi]
.

Proposition 5.9
.

Let
\pi
be a basic representation of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
(resp.
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
,
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n+1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
). Assume that
\ell
be the first descent index of
\pi
. Let
\pi^{\prime}
be an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2(n-\ell)}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
(resp.
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2(n-\ell)-1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
,
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2(n-\ell)}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
) such that
m_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then
\pi^{\prime}
is basic.

Proof.

The cuspidal case for symplectic groups has been proven in
[
PW1
, Theorem 6.16]
. It is easy to extend the result to basic representations. The proof for the orthogonal group follows a similar approach
∎

Proposition 5.10
.

(i) Let
\pi=\pi_{\rho}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
. Assume that
\ell
be the first descent index of
\pi
. Let
\pi^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2(n-\ell)})
such that
m_{\epsilon}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\neq 0
. Then we have
m_{-\epsilon}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\neq 0
and
\pi^{\prime}={\pi_{\rho^{-}_{1}}}
(see (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) for the definition of
\rho_{1}^{-}
).

(ii) Let
\pi=\pi_{\rho}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n})
(resp.
\pi_{\rho,\iota}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{cus}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n+1})
). Assume that
\ell
be the first descent index of
\pi
. Let
\pi^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2(n-\ell)-1})
(resp.
{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2n-\ell})
) such that
m_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\neq 0
with
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}=\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}
(resp.
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}=\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}
). Then we have
\pi^{\prime}=\pi_{\rho_{1},{\widetilde{\iota}}_{\rho_{1}}}
(resp.
\pi_{\rho_{1}}
).

Proof.

The symplectic has been proved in
[
PW1
, Theorem 6.3]
. The orthogonal case follows from the symplectic case and see-saw identity. When
\pi^{\prime}
is a representation of a odd orthogonal group, we obtain
\pi^{\prime}(-I)
by Proposition
4.5
.
∎

Proposition 5.11
.

(1)

Let
\pi=\pi_{\rho,k,h}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
. Assume that
\ell
be the first descent index of
\pi
. Let
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2(n-\ell)})
. If
m_{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})\neq 0
for some
\epsilon\in\{\pm\}
, then we have

•

(i)
\rho^{\prime}=\rho^{-}_{1}
;

•

(ii)
|k^{\prime}|=\lceil|h|-1\rceil
;

•

(iii)
|h^{\prime}|=|k^{\prime}|-1
.

(2)

Let
\pi=\pi_{\rho,k,h}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n})
(resp.
\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n+1})
).
Assume that
\ell
be the first descent index of
\pi
. Let
\pi^{\prime}=\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2(n-\ell)-1})
(resp.
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2(n-\ell)}
). If
m_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\neq 0
with
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}=\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}
(resp.
\epsilon^{\prime\prime}=\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}
), then we have

•

(i)
\rho^{\prime}=\rho_{1}
;

•

(ii)
|k^{\prime}|=\lceil|k|-1\rceil
;

•

(iii)
|h^{\prime}|=\lceil|h|-1\rceil
.

Proof.

We only prove (1). The proof of (2) is similar, and left it to readers.
By
[
Wang1
, Theorem 1.1]
we know that
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
are
\epsilon
-strongly relevant for some
\epsilon
, and
m_{\pm}(\pi^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\rho},\pi^{\mathrm{Sp}}_{\rho^{\prime}})\neq 0
.
Now, let
\pi^{\star}=\pi_{\rho^{\star},k^{\star},h^{\star}}
such that

•

(i)
\rho^{\star}=\rho^{\prime}
;

•

(ii)
|h^{\star}|=|k|-1
;

•

(iii)
|k^{\star}|=\lceil|h|-1\rceil
.

According to Proposition
4.10
, there exists
\epsilon^{\star}
such that one of
(\pi,\pi^{\star})
and
(\pi,(\pi^{\star})^{c})
is
\epsilon^{\star}
-strongly relevant.
Hence, by
[
Wang1
, Theorem 1.1]
, either
m_{\epsilon^{\star}}(\pi,\pi^{\star})\neq 0
or
m_{\epsilon^{\star}}(\pi,(\pi^{\star})^{c})\neq 0
. By Corollary
5.7
, if
m_{\epsilon^{\prime\prime}}(\pi,\pi^{\prime})\neq 0
, then
|h^{\prime}|\geq|h^{\star}|
and
|k^{\prime}|\geq|k^{\star}|
.
Since
\ell
is the first descent, we have
|k^{\prime}|=|k^{\star}|
,
|h^{\prime}|=|h^{\star}|
, and
\rho^{\prime}
should appear in the first descent of
\rho
. By Proposition
5.10
, we know that
\rho^{\prime}=\rho^{-}_{1}
.
∎

Although we can not explicitly describe which irreducible representation appears in the first descent of an irreducible basic representation
\pi
by Proposition
5.11
, we find a irreducible basic representation
\pi^{\prime}
such that
[\pi^{\prime}]
contains all irreducible constituent of first descents of
[\pi]
. Similar to theta correspondence, we are going to prove conservation relation of first descents for basic representations, which allows us to determined which representation in
[\pi^{\prime}]
appears in the descent of
\pi
.

Theorem 5.12
.

Let
\pi_{\rho,k,h}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
. Let
\ell
be the first descent index of
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
. Assume that
h\neq 0
and
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
appears in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{F}J}_{\ell,\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})
. Then we have

•

(i)
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\ell,-\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})=0
;

•

(ii)
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\ell,\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c})=0
;

•

(iii)
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\ell,-\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c})=\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}^{c}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.1
and
[
Sze
, Proposition 11]
,
we have

(5.7)

\displaystyle m_{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})=

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{\rho,k,h},\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})\otimes\omega^{\epsilon}_{n}\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c},\left(\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})\otimes\omega_{n}^{\epsilon}\right)^{c}\right\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{\rho,k,-h},\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau^{c}\otimes\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},-h^{\prime}})\otimes\omega_{n}^{-\epsilon}\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle m_{-\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,-h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},-h^{\prime}}),

where
P
is a parabolic subgroup of
\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}
with Levi factor
\mathrm{GL}_{\ell}\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2(n-\ell)}
, and
\tau\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{GL}_{\ell})
is defined in Proposition
5.1
. In particular, we can pick a
\tau
such that
\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})
is irreducible. This completes the proof of (iii).

Let
n^{\pm}
be the first theta occurrence index of
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{odd}}^{\pm}
. By Proposition
4.10
(ii), there exists
{\epsilon^{\star}}
such that
n^{\epsilon^{\star}}<n
, the theta lifting
\Theta^{\epsilon^{\star}}_{n,n^{\epsilon^{\star}}}(\pi)
is an irreducible cuspidal representation, and we denote it by
\pi^{\star}=\pi_{\rho^{\star},k^{\star},h^{\star},\iota^{\star}}
.
On the other hand, we know that
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
occurs in
\Theta^{\epsilon^{\star}}_{n^{\epsilon^{\star}},n}(\pi^{\star})
.
Consider the see-saw diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.4295pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.4295pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 66.35316pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon{\epsilon^{\star}}}_{2(n^{\epsilon^{\star}}+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.2703pt\raise-33.47308pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 51.4295pt\raise-33.47308pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\star}}_{2n^{\epsilon^{\star}}+1}\times\mathrm{O}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon}_{1}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Even though we are considering Ma-Qiu-Zou’s Weil representation instead of Gérardin’s, the see-saw identity still remains valid.
We have

(5.8)

\displaystyle m_{\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})=

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{\rho,k,h}\otimes\omega_{n}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon},\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\langle\Theta^{\epsilon^{\star}}_{n^{\epsilon^{\star}},n}(\pi^{\star}),\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{\rho^{\star},k^{\star},h^{\star},\iota^{\star}},\Theta^{\epsilon\epsilon^{\star}}_{n,n^{\epsilon^{\star}}+1}(\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}))\rangle_{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}.

Therefore,

\Theta^{\epsilon\epsilon^{\star}}_{n,n^{\epsilon^{\star}}+1}(\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}))\neq 0,

and by Proposition
4.1
, we know that
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
goes down with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{even}}^{\epsilon\epsilon^{\star}}
.

By Proposition
5.11
, if
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{F}J}_{\ell,-\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})\neq 0
, then it consists of some of
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
and
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},-h^{\prime}}
. First, we prove that
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
does not appear in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{F}J}_{\ell,-\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})
.
Consider the the see-saw diagram

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.4295pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.4295pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 67.7004pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon{\epsilon^{\star}}}_{2(n^{\epsilon^{\star}}+1)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-13.2703pt\raise-33.47308pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 51.4295pt\raise-33.47308pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{-\epsilon^{\star}}_{2n^{\epsilon^{\star}}+1}\times\mathrm{O}^{-\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon}_{1}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We can use the same see-saw argument to conclude that if
m_{-\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})\neq 0
, then

\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
goes down with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{even}}^{-\epsilon\epsilon^{\star}}
. However, we already proved that
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
goes down with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{even}}^{\epsilon\epsilon^{\star}}
. Therefore
by conservation relation,
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
has to go up with respect to the Witt tower
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{even}}^{-\epsilon\epsilon^{\star}}
. Hence we have

m_{-\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}})=0.

By Theorem
4.10
, the first theta occurrence indexes of
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},-h^{\prime}}
and
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
with respect to the Witt towers
{\bf{{O}}}_{\mathrm{even}}^{\pm}
are the same. Using the same see-saw argument again, we have
m_{-\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},-h^{\prime}})=0
. So
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{F}J}_{\ell,-\epsilon}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})
contains neither
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
nor
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},-h^{\prime}}
, which proves (i). We can conclude (ii) from (i) and (
5.7
).
∎

Theorem 5.13
.

Let
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
be an irreducible basic representation
\mathrm{O}_{2n}^{\epsilon}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. Assume that
k,h\neq 0
. Let
\ell
be the first descent index of
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
. Assume that
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
appears in
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})
for some
{\epsilon^{\prime}}
. Then we have

(i)

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k,h})=\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
;

(ii)

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2n}^{\epsilon}}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k,h})=\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2(n-\ell)-1}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\cdot\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
;

(iii)

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c})=\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
;

(iv)

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})=0
;

(v)

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c})=0
,

where
\chi_{G}
is the spinor norm of
G^{F}
.

Proof.

The statements (i) and (ii) are obvious, and (v) follows from (iii) and (iv). Let us prove (iii). By Proposition
5.2
, for any
\pi^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{O}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}}_{2n-2\ell-1})
, we have

(5.9)

\displaystyle m_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\pi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=m_{{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h},\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{O}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}}_{2n-1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=\langle\pi_{\rho,k,h},\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{O}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}}_{2n-1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathrm{O}_{2n}^{\epsilon}(\mathbb{F}_{q})},

where
P
and
\tau
are defined in Proposition
5.2
.
Let
g
be a element of
\mathrm{CO}_{2n}^{\epsilon}
sending
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
to
\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c}
. Then
{}^{g}(\mathrm{O}_{2n-1}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}})
is the isometry group of a
(2n-1)
-dimensional symmetric space
V
with
{\mathrm{disc}}(V)=-\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}
, and
g
sends
\pi^{\prime}
to an irreducible representation
{}^{g}\pi^{\prime}
of
{}^{g}(\mathrm{O}_{2n-1}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}})(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. By direct calculation,
\pi^{\prime}
and
{}^{g}\pi^{\prime}
are the same as irreducible representations of
\mathrm{O}_{2n-1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
. By abuse of notations, we denote these two irreducible representations by
\pi^{\prime}
.
Applying the conjugate action of
g
on (
5.9
), we have

\displaystyle\langle\pi_{\rho,k,h},\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{O}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}}_{2n-1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathrm{O}_{2n}^{\epsilon}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}=\langle\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c},\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathrm{O}^{-\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}}_{2n-1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}_{P^{\prime}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}(\tau\otimes\pi^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathrm{O}_{2n}^{\epsilon}(\mathbb{F}_{q})}=m_{-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c},\pi^{\prime})

where
P^{\prime}={}^{g}P
.
Hence
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h}^{c})=\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
.

We now turn to prove (iv).
Assume that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})\neq 0
. Then by Proposition
5.11
,
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})
equals either
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
or
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},-\iota^{\prime}}
. Consider the following see-saw diagrams

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 30.13438pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.13438pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n^{\prime}}\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2n^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.75621pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 140.58528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\quad\quad}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 190.58531pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n^{\prime}}\times\mathrm{Sp}_{2n^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 298.55925pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-14.62274pt\raise-32.6289pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 54.13438pt\raise-32.6289pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}}_{2n-1}\times\mathrm{O}^{{\epsilon^{\prime}}}_{1}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 140.58528pt\raise-32.6289pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\quad\quad}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 207.4494pt\raise-32.6289pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 274.85408pt\raise-32.6289pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{O}^{-\varepsilon(-1)\epsilon{\epsilon^{\prime}}}_{2n-1}\times\mathrm{O}^{-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}_{1}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Since
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})=\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
, the representations
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
and
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
are both go up or go down in the first see-saw diagram. Then by Proposition
4.11
,
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
and
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},-\iota^{\prime}}
go in a different direction in the first see-saw diagram.
Recall that the first theta occurrence indexes of irreducible representations of odd orthogonal groups are the same with respect to two different Witt tower
{\bf{O}}^{\pm}_{\mathrm{odd}}
. We know that
\pi_{\rho,k,h}
and
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},-\iota^{\prime}}
go in a different direction in the second see-saw diagram, and
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})
can not be
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},-\iota^{\prime}}
.

On the other hand,
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{2\ell,-a}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})
can not be
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
either.
By (ii), we have

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2n}^{\epsilon}}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k,h})=\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2(n-\ell)-1}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\cdot\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}.

As irreducible representations of
\mathrm{O}_{2(n-\ell)-1}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
,

\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2(n-\ell)-1}^{-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}}\cdot\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}\cong\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2(n-\ell)-1}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\cdot\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}.

Applying the same see-saw argument on
\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2(n-\ell)-1}^{-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}}\cdot\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
, we have

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2n}^{\epsilon}}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k,h})\neq\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2(n-\ell)-1}^{-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}}\cdot\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}.

and hence

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})\neq\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}.

So
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h})
is neither
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}}
nor
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{1}^{\prime},\Lambda_{-1}^{\prime},-\epsilon^{\prime}}
.
∎

Similarly, we have the following result for odd orthogonal groups.

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{bas}}(\mathrm{O}_{2n+1}^{\epsilon})
. Assume that
k,h\neq 1
. Let
\ell
be the first descent index of
\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}
. Assume that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=\pi_{\rho^{\prime},k^{\prime},h^{\prime}}
for some
{\epsilon^{\prime}}
. Then

(i)

We have
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=0
.

(ii)

If we recognize
\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota}
as an irreducible representation of
\mathrm{O}_{2n+1}^{-{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\mathbb{F}_{q})
, we have

{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,\pm{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=0
;

(iii)

We have
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k,h}
;

(iv)

We have
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,{\epsilon^{\prime}}}(\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2n+1}^{\epsilon}}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k,h,\iota})=\chi_{\mathrm{O}_{2n-\ell}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}}\cdot\pi_{\rho,k,h}
.

Combing our statements of first descents and first theta lifting for basic representations, we have the following commutative diagram

(5.10)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 32.53853pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-30.55237pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern where

•

\Theta
is the first theta lifting;

•

m=n-\ell
(resp.
m^{\prime}=n^{\prime}-\ell^{\prime}
),
\ell
(resp.
\ell^{\prime}
) is the first descent index of
\pi
(resp.
\pi^{\prime}
);

•

{\mathcal{D}}_{\ell,\epsilon}
(resp.
{\mathcal{D}}_{\ell^{\prime},\epsilon}
) is
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{FJ}}_{\ell,\epsilon}
or
{\mathcal{D}}^{\mathrm{B}}_{\ell,\epsilon}
, it depends on
G_{n}
(resp.
G^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}}
);

•

n^{\prime}
is the first occurrence index of
\pi
.

Remark 5.15
.

As mentioned in Remark
5.3
, all statements in this subsection apply to quadratic unipotent representations when
q
is odd.

6.
Construction of canonical choice of Lusztig correspondence

6.1.
The canonical choice of Lusztig correspondence

The goal of this section is to prove our main theorem.

Theorem 6.1
.

Suppose that the cardinality of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
is large
enough so that the main result in
[
Sr1
]
holds. Let
G_{n}=\mathrm{Sp}_{2n}
,
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n}
or
\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon}_{2n+1}
.
One has a unique choice of Lusztig correspondence
\cal{L}^{\mathrm{c}an}
satisfying the following conditions.

(1)

It is compatible with parabolic induction (cf. Section
3.4
).

(2)

Consider the dual pair
(G_{n},G^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}})=(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n},\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2n^{\prime}+1})
.
Let
\pi=\pi_{\rho,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{-1}}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
, and
\pi^{\prime}=\pi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime}_{1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{-1},\iota^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2n^{\prime}+1})
. Then
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime}_{1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{-1},\iota^{\prime}}
occurs in
\Theta(\pi_{\rho,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{-1}})
if and only if the following conditions hold:

•

For any
a\neq\pm 1
in
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
,
G^{*}_{[a]}\cong(G^{\prime})^{*}_{[-a]}
and
\pi[a]\cong\pi^{\prime}[-a]
where
\rho=\prod\pi[a]
, and
\rho^{\prime}=\prod\pi^{\prime}[a]
;

•

\Lambda_{-1}^{\prime}=\Lambda_{1}
;

•

\pi_{\Lambda^{\prime}_{1}}
occurs in
\Theta_{\epsilon^{\prime}\epsilon_{\rho}}(\pi_{\Lambda_{-1}})
;

•

\iota^{\prime}={\widetilde{\iota}}_{\rho}\mathrm{pd}(\Lambda_{-1})\varepsilon(-1)^{\mathrm{rank}(\Lambda_{1})}
,

where

\Theta_{\epsilon^{\prime}\epsilon_{\rho}}(\pi):=\begin{cases}\Theta(\pi),&\textrm{if }\epsilon^{\prime}\epsilon_{\rho}=+;\\
\Theta(\mathrm{sgn}\cdot\pi),&\textrm{otherwise}.\end{cases}

In other words, we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\pi_{\rho,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{-1}}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{c}an}}
\textstyle{\pi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime}_{1},\Lambda^{\prime}_{-1},\iota^{\prime}}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2n^{\prime}+1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{c}an}}
\textstyle{\rho\otimes\pi_{\Lambda_{-1}}\otimes\pi_{\Lambda_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{iso}\otimes\Theta_{\epsilon^{\prime}\epsilon_{\rho}}\otimes{\mathcal{L}}_{1}}
\textstyle{\rho^{\prime}\otimes\pi_{\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}}\otimes\pi_{\Lambda^{\prime}_{-1}}\otimes\iota^{\prime},}

where the first isomorphism sends
\rho
to
\rho^{\prime}
by sending
\pi[a]
to
\pi^{\prime}[-a]
, and
{\mathcal{L}}_{1}
is the Lusztig correspondence between unipotent representations.

(3)

Consider the dual pair
(G_{n},G_{n^{\prime}}^{\prime})=(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n},\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2n^{\prime}})
. For
\pi=\pi_{\rho,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{-1}}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})
, and
\pi^{\prime}=\pi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{1}^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime}_{-1}}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{O}^{\epsilon^{\prime}}_{2n^{\prime}})
. Then
\pi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{1}^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime}_{-1}}
occurs in
\Theta(\pi_{\rho,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{-1}})
if and only if the following conditions hold:

•

For any
a\neq\pm 1
in
\bar{\mathbb{F}_{q}}
,
G^{*}_{[a]}\cong(G^{\prime})^{*}_{[a]}
and
\pi^{\prime}[a]\cong\pi[a]
where
\rho=\prod\pi[a]
, and
\rho^{\prime}=\prod\pi^{\prime}[a]
;

•

\Lambda_{-1}^{\prime}=\Lambda_{-1}
;

•

\pi_{\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}}
occurs in
\Theta(\pi_{\Lambda_{1}})
.

In other words, we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\pi_{\rho,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{-1}}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{Sp}_{2n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{c}an}}
\textstyle{\pi_{\rho^{\prime},\Lambda_{1}^{\prime},\Lambda^{\prime}_{-1}}\in{\mathcal{E}}(\mathrm{O}_{2n^{\prime}}^{\epsilon^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{c}an}}
\textstyle{\rho\otimes\pi_{\Lambda_{1}}\otimes\pi_{\Lambda_{-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{iso}\otimes\Theta\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\textstyle{\rho\otimes\pi_{\Lambda_{1}^{\prime}}\otimes\pi_{\Lambda_{-1}}.}

Proof.

By our discussion in section
3.4
, we know that
{\cal{L}^{\mathrm{can}}}
is uniquely determined by condition (1) and its behavior on basic representations.

Consider conditions (2) and (3).
By Proposition
3.8
and Proposition
4.5
, we have

\iota^{\prime}=\pi_{\rho,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{-1}}(-I)\epsilon(-1)^{n}=\iota_{\rho}\mathrm{pd}(\Lambda_{-1})\varepsilon(-1)^{n-\mathrm{rank}(\Lambda_{-1})}.

Note that
n=|\rho|+\mathrm{rank}(\Lambda_{1})+\mathrm{rank}(\Lambda_{-1})
. Then

\iota^{\prime}=\pi_{\rho,\Lambda_{1},\Lambda_{-1}}(-I)\epsilon(-1)^{n}=\iota_{\rho}\mathrm{pd}(\Lambda_{-1})\varepsilon(-1)^{|\rho|+\mathrm{rank}(\Lambda_{1})}={\widetilde{\iota}}_{\rho}\mathrm{pd}(\Lambda_{-1})\varepsilon(-1)^{\mathrm{rank}(\Lambda_{1})}.

Theorem
4.3
and Theorem
4.4
state that the requirement of
\rho
holds, and the symbol part requirement holds up to the transpose.
If a symbol equals its transpose, then there is nothing left to prove. We now assume that
\Lambda_{\pm 1}
and
\Lambda^{\prime}_{\pm 1}
are not equal to their transpose, respectively. As discussed in Section
3.3
, we can find basic representations
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
such that
\pi\in{\mathcal{E}}(G_{n},\sigma)
and
\pi^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}(G^{\prime}_{n^{\prime}},\sigma^{\prime})
. Using the same argument in the proof of
[
AMR
, Théorèm 3.7]
and
[
LW1
, Theorem 4.1]
, we conclude that
\pi^{\prime}
occurs in
\Theta(\pi)
implies that
\sigma^{\prime}
occurs in
\Theta(\sigma)
. In other words, the theta lifting the parabolic induction are compatible. It’s important to note that we are dealing with Ma-Qiu-Zou’s Weil representation, not Gerardin’s. Therefore, we should refer to
[
MQZ
, Proposition 2.1]
instead of
[
MVW
, Chap. 3, IV th.5]
to prove this compatibility. According to this, we only need to demonstrate that there exists a
{\cal{L}^{\mathrm{can}}}
that satisfies the conditions (2) and (3) for basic representations. The proof of the basic case is provided in section
6.3
and section
6.3
.
The uniqueness of
{\cal{L}^{\mathrm{can}}}
on basic representations follows from the fact that we can resolve the ambiguity in the Lusztig correspondence using the first occurrence of theta lifting (see section
4.5
).

∎

Remark 6.2
.

The only place in the proof of Theorem
6.1
we use the assumption of
q
is when we refer to the conclusions in section
5
.
As in Remark
5.15
, Theorem
6.1
apply to quadratic unipotent representations when
q
is odd. In other words, there exists a unique Lusztig correspondence
{\cal{L}^{\mathrm{can}}}
on quadratic unipotent representations satisfying the above conditions.

6.2.
Strategy for the proof of the basic representation Theorem

We have already established that there is commutativity between theta correspondence and the first descent through a see-saw diagram. It is important to note that we aim to create a choice
{{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{can}}}
of Lusztig that is commutative with theta correspondence. Assuming that such a standard choice
{{\mathcal{L}}^{\mathrm{can}}}
exists, we are looking to identify functors
{\mathcal{D}}^{*}_{\pm}
so that the diagram below is commutative.

(6.1)
